User decisions: they own **game D** (gyro + rumble — the game for their check; tilt and light checked by tests and Claude); **light = the phone's sensor + a manual slider** (☀ button on the game screen for solar carts, Auto = sensor); **motion keys too** (I/J/K/L tilt ±24000, Q/E turn ±16000, as `gbaview`); **real phone at the end** (their phone, installed only with their OK via new `dev.sh phone-install`/`phone-uninstall`, one non-emulator device or refuse; tests never run there); **sensors read in Kotlin** (option 1 of 3: `MotionMath` pure maths — tilt = gravity per screen edge, 1 g = full, flat = level; turn = gyro z, clockwise +, 4π rad/s = full; light = 255 × log10(lux/10)/4; keys override their axis — `MotionInput` sensors only for the cart's extras, `SENSOR_DELAY_GAME`, off in the background; `Rumble` Choreographer poll → VibratorManager/Vibrator; C stores atomics applied at frame start). Own test cartridges by game code (the core matches 3 letters): `hw_gyro` (RZWT: gyro + rumble, motor on/off every 30 frames), `hw_tilt` (KYGT), `hw_solar` (U3IT). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-milestone-4d-rumble-sensors-design.md`.

- **M4d prototype (2026-09-26)** — the user approved the spec ('It's good, continue'); trial version first. Worktree `<scratchpad>/p4d`, branch `proto-4d` (commit `f709342`; delete at the end of 4d). Built: `tools/build_test_roms.py` reads `@ game code: XXXX` from a source (default ZOWN; sources read as UTF-8 — an existing one has a non-ASCII character); own ROMs `hw_gyro.S` (RZWT: gyro + rumble; motor on 30 / off 30 frames), `hw_tilt.S` (KYGT), `hw_solar.S` (U3IT) — the existing own ROMs rebuild byte-identical; `emu_thread` atomics tilt/turn/light applied at frame start via `apply_sensors` (the core's setters only store values), rumble state after each frame, `emu_cart_hardware`, hook `emu_motion_seen`; JNI + `NativeBridge` `setMotion`, `setLight`, `cartHardware`, `rumbleActive`, `HW_*`, `debugMotionSeen`; Kotlin `MotionMath` (+ `RumbleSwitch`), `MotionInput` (sensors per the cart's extras, `SENSOR_DELAY_GAME`, keys, manual light, debug log `motion: tilt x y turn t light l` ≤ 1/s), `Rumble` (Choreographer poll, 5 s one-shot renewed, cancelled on change/pause/Back/quit), ☀ button + slider with Auto in `GameScreen` (solar carts), `MainActivity` wiring (motion keys I/J/K/L/Q/E before GBA keys), manifest `VIBRATE`, `dev.sh phone-install` / `phone-uninstall` / `phone-push` (exactly one non-emulator device, else refuse — checked with only the emulator: refused, exit 1). Tests: Kotlin 24 (+10 `MotionMathTest`), PC 363, CTest 56/56, on-device 33 (+4 `MotionTest`), 0 C warnings. **Found while prototyping:** (1) **rumble never vibrated** — evidence: the Choreographer poll ran but `rumbleActive()` was ~always false; a temporary test showed the motor on in 1 of ~315 samples with or without a save path, so the first `MotionTest` ('seen on once') passed by luck. Root cause: `hw_gyro.S` made the GPIO registers readable, and `wait_frames` (common.inc) sits right at 0x080000C0 — so the CPU then fetched the GPIO registers at 0xC4–0xC9 instead of its code and derailed after the first frame. Fix: the ROM leaves the registers unreadable (rumble needs no reads; comment in the source); `MotionTest` now requires the motor on 35–65 % of 2 s of samples (got 155/314). (2) `adb emu sensor set acceleration …` works for the check, but my first run tilted the virtual device into landscape (auto-rotate) — set `accelerometer_rotation 0` first. Claude's adb check: `hw_tilt` — `adb emu sensor set acceleration -4.9:0:8.5` → `tilt 16372 0`, 0:-4.9 → `0 -16372`, L key → 24000; `hw_gyro` — gyroscope 0:0:-6.28 → `turn 16375`, +3.14 → −8188; `dumpsys vibrator_manager`: 0.5 s one-shots every second from `com.tengen.gbaemu`, none after Back; `hw_solar` — light 1,000 / 100,000 / 5 lux → 128 / 255 / 0, ☀ opens the slider ('Light: sensor', Auto on), a tap on the slider → `light 235 (manual)`, Auto → back to the sensor; the panel sits bottom-left (over the D-pad when sideways, while open). game D ROM not found in the user's Downloads — asked the user.

- **M4d trial by the user (2026-09-27)** — the user gave their ROM `~\Downloads\game-d.gba` (16 MiB, header `RZWE` → gyro + rumble). **Emulator incident:** overnight (the PC slept) the emulator's Android broke — `init.svc.zygote` = `restarting`, system services missing, `/sdcard` 'Transport endpoint is not connected'; `logcat -b crash`: `FATAL EXCEPTION IN SYSTEM PROCESS` — `IllegalStateException: Invalid @IntRange(from = -1): -39877` in `AppOpsService` / `Notifier.notifyWakelockRelease` (a negative wakelock duration: an Android bug after the clock jump), all other crashes 'The system died'. Not our app. `adb reboot` — **the user rejected it** (then said continue); they had closed the emulator; `dev.sh emu` started it but quick boot restored the same broken snapshot (its clock still 13:13:20). User decisions: fast-forward **tap or hold** (tap < 300 ms flips it on/off, a hold is fast while held then off); **speed in the in-game menu 2×/4×/8×/Max** (a row cycling them, remembered for all games, default 4×); **sound sped up** while fast (higher pitch), **silent at Max**; rewind reaches **~3 minutes** (64 MiB, a snapshot every 4 frames), held ⟲, silent, ~4× backwards; **⟲ ⏩ at the top between L and R** (layout A, visual companion); **rewind in the core with our own packer** (XOR with the previous snapshot → zero runs + literals; no LZ4 download); save-state files stay unpacked. Gamepad R2 = fast-forward, L2 = rewind; two keyboard keys to be found while prototyping (letters arrive as taps). Proof: `testrunner --rewind-at <f>:<s>` + 32 CTest `rewind_*` (88 → 120) and the same on the emulator. **Known risk:** zero-run packing may reach less than 3 minutes (the framebuffer and sound buffer change every frame) — the prototype measures it in game A. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-milestone-5b-fast-forward-rewind-design.md`. The visual companion ran at `.superpowers/brainstorm/3919-1790551417` (gitignored).

- **M5b prototype (2026-09-27)** — the user approved the spec ('It's good, build the prototype'). Worktree `<this session's scratchpad>/p5b` (= `~/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Tengen-Documents-gba-emulator/756fa686-5b2d-4ed5-931f-270af7dd2c77/scratchpad/p5b`), branch `proto-5b` (delete at the end of 5b; its `tests/roms` is a copy, gitignored). Built: core `delta_pack.{h,c}` (records of unchanged count / changed count as varints + XOR bytes; runs of < 8 unchanged bytes stay inside the changed ones; bound 2n+2), `rewind.{h,c}` (ring of records with the length at both ends, `current` kept in full; capture at the end of `gba_run_frame` every 4 frames; step back loads `current` first if frames ran since it, else unpacks the newest into it; the capture counter restarts at the restored moment), `gba_rewind_configure/step_back` in `gba.h`, `state_load` (internal, keeps the history) vs `gba_state_load` (clears it), `gba_reset` clears, `gba_debug_rewind_stats`; `testrunner --rewind-at f:s` (audio hash rolled back to before the restored frame's sound, which is still unread in the snapshot) + CMake `rewind_<name>` (f = 2/3 of the frames, short runs frames−1; steps = f/4/2, at least 2 so every run unpacks) → **CTest 120/120**; PC `test_delta_pack.c` (5) + `test_rewind.c` (6) → **380**. **Sabotage:** an unpacker that skips the first byte of each changed run failed 5 of 32 (the 3 own sound ROMs, tonc_snd1_demo, tonc_pageflip); still pictures don't show it, the unit tests do. Android: `emu_set_fast_forward` (2/4/8: N frames per loop step, resampler ratio ÷ N → sped-up sound, sound pacing unchanged; `EMU_FF_MAX` = -1: no pacing, sound dropped), `emu_set_rewinding` (one step per 1/60 s by the clock, sound dropped, keys ignored; on release the save timer is marked), rewind configured in `emu_create` (64 MiB / 4), `emu_rewind_stats`; JNI `setFastForward`, `setRewinding`, `debugRunFramesViaRewind`, `debugRewindStats`; androidTest `RewindTest` (4: 32 fingerprints through a rewind; 2×/4×/Max by the clock; 2× paced by sound; rewinding to the oldest snapshot runs no frames, then plays on) → **on-device 41/41**. Kotlin: `FastForwardSwitch` (+ JVM test, 5), `KeyMap.FAST`/`REWIND` (bits 10/11, `GBA_BUTTONS` = 0x3FF; Tab / ` and gamepad R2 / L2 — shown in Controls), `TouchLayout` (⟲ ⏩ between L and R upright; **sideways below L and R** — between them would cover the game), `TouchControlsView` (⏩ lit yellow while switched on; symbols 20 dp), menu row 'Fast-forward: 4×' cycling 2×/4×/8×/Max (prefs `settings`/`fast_speed`) → **Kotlin 31**. 0 C warnings. **Measured reach** (scratch tool `<scratchpad>/poke/measure.c` with the user's ROM, .sav and auto.state copied read-only from the emulator — the app's own files untouched): ~27 KB per snapshot walking → **~2 min 40 s** at 64 MiB; PC 461 fps with captures; a step back ~0.02 ms. Claude's adb check (Mode 7 demo): buttons drawn upright and sideways, a tap lights ⏩, the menu row cycles 4×→8×→Max→2×→4× (left at 4×), no crash. The emulator had closed after the test run and was started again with `dev.sh emu`. **Still to check with the user:** whether Tab and ` arrive as real (holdable) presses from the PC keyboard in the emulator window (letters don't). Note: the app on the emulator is the prototype build; its Continue now points at m7_demo (opened for the check).

- **M5b trial, round 1 (2026-09-27):** the user: 'Everything works up until step 5' — Tab and ` held in the emulator window act as taps (Tab toggles). Recorded with `getevent -lt` while the user held keys (a laptop: no Home/End; Right Shift sent nothing): **Space (like every letter, Tab, `) = a 0.1 ms tap, then after the PC's repeat delay (~500 ms) a tap every ~33 ms; the arrows = a real press for 500 ms, then release/press pairs every ~33 ms.** So no laptop key holds cleanly. The user asked for different keys → **F = fast-forward, R = rewind**, read through a new `KeyRepeatFilter` (pure, JVM test 5): a key-up ends the hold only if no key-down follows within 60 ms, or 600 ms after a tap-like (< 10 ms) press for ⏩ (bridges the repeat delay, so a held F is not taken for a tap; the tap/hold decision uses the key-up's time); ⟲ uses 60 ms only (no overshoot; may pause ~0.5 s right after pressing R). Gamepad R2/L2 and touch unchanged. Kotlin 36. Prototype commit on `proto-5b`.

- **M5b trial, round 2 (2026-09-27):** R works; a held F still ended in fast mode. Debugged with logging in the app + `getevent` while the user held F (systematic debugging): **the PC auto-repeats only the last key pressed** — holding F and pressing an arrow to walk stops F's repeat stream at once (one run: F tapped, ← 0.35 s later, before F's first repeat → a lone tap → the switch flips on); letter keys never send a key-up at the real release. So a held letter key while walking cannot be seen through the emulator window. Held on its own, F was handled right (hold → off). **User decision (option 1 of 2):** on the laptop F is a tap switch (tap on / tap off); holding stays for the touch ⏩, gamepad R2 and real keyboards on a phone; the 600 ms first-repeat wait was removed (it could not help; taps now react at once) — `KeyRepeatFilter` keeps only the 60 ms gap. Logging removed. Kotlin 35. (Rejected: hunting a non-letter key such as Delete / Page Down — another round, and laptops may lack them.)

- **M5b Task 1** — `core/src/delta_pack.{h,c}`: the difference of two equal-sized snapshots as records (unchanged-byte count, changed-byte count as varints, then the changed bytes XORed); runs of < 8 unchanged bytes stay inside the changed ones; `delta_pack_bound(n)` = 2n + 2; `delta_unpack` XORs straight into the buffer and refuses input that does not describe exactly n bytes. Built so far (each step: 410 unit tests, CTest 120/120, the 6 scene hashes `ok` on the PC and phone B): (0) `GBA_FORCE_GENERIC` (core/CMakeLists.txt; `GBA_ARM64` defined when the arm64 fast paths are in; `core/arch/arm64/` empty so far), the unit tests built for the phone (`GBA_BENCH_ONLY` adds tests/unit), `dev.sh phone-unit [filter]` (runs in `/data/local/tmp/gbabench` — the save-file tests write in the current folder) and `GENERIC=1` for the phone commands (`build/phone-arm64-generic`, Gradle `-PgbaForceGeneric`) — **410/410 on phone B both ways**; found: a `set -e` script line `X="$( [ … ] && echo …)"` silently ended dev.sh. (1) `cpu_run` + an inline fetch (`mem/bus_inline.h`). (2) Specialised ARM data-processing handlers (one copy per opcode/S/operand kind). (3) Specialised Thumb handler copies (`thumb_spec.py`), `cpu_cond_passed` inline. (4) **A threaded C loop** (`cpu/cpu_fast.h`, `__attribute__((musttail))`): fast handlers for data processing, B/BL, LDR/STR(B) with RAM/ROM, the Thumb ALU/immediate/shift/hi-register forms, B<cond>, B, BL, the Thumb loads/stores; each bails out before any side effect. (5) A generic in-loop handler for every other instruction (as cpu_step does it), so nothing leaves the loop except an event due, an interrupt to take, a halt, a state change or code outside RAM/ROM. (6) Fast LDM/STM, PUSH/POP, LDMIA/STMIA with RAM.

Profiles on phone B after (3): game A CPU loop ~45 %, `ppu_compose` 18 %, `ppu_render_text_bg` 18 %; game B CPU loop ~75 %. (7) **The ARM64 assembly loop** (`core/arch/arm64/cpu_a64.S` + `cpu_a64.c`; `cpu_run` → `a64_cpu_run` on arm64, `cpu_run_generic` = the C loop elsewhere and with `GBA_FORCE_GENERIC`): guest PC, cycle count, limit (next event or an interrupt becoming visible, one number), the sequential flag and a code "window" (host pointer + range, refreshed in C) in registers; ARM: data processing (imm and imm-shift operands, 150 macro copies), B/BL, LDR/STR(B) with RAM/ROM; Thumb: shifts, add/sub, imm8, ALU (not MUL), hi registers, LDR [PC], all load/store forms with RAM/ROM, ADD PC/SP, ADD SP, B<cond>, B, BL; everything else through `a64_slow` (cpu_step), the prefetch stall through `a64_stall` (bus_prefetch_stall). (8) **Twin tests** `tests/unit/test_cpu_twins.c` (random ARM and Thumb programs in IWRAM and in the ROM, random flags / WAITCNT / prefetch; compared every 3000 cycles: registers, banks, flags, now, all RAM/VRAM/OAM/palette, I/O, IRQ, DMA, timers): the C loop vs cpu_step (PC and phone), the asm loop vs the C loop (phone only) — **414 unit tests on phone B**; planted bugs caught: the asm's logical-op C flag, the asm's stall off_rom count, the C Thumb loads' idle cycle, the asm Thumb ADC carry. Found: a64_window said yes for Thumb code in RAM's last halfword (outside its window) → an endless loop; fixed.
  After (7): game A 2.84, game B 2.45, game C 4.37, game D 2.36, m7 4.56, snd1 15.23 (all hashes ok). (9) LDM/STM, PUSH/POP, LDMIA/STMIA in asm (planted bugs caught: POP's idle cycle, STM's stored-base rule) → 2.94/2.57/4.62/2.46/4.95/16.44. (10) The palette, VRAM and OAM in the fast data paths (C and asm; not byte stores; VRAM's mirror), the code window kept across slow instructions (same WAITCNT and state), the twin tests' bases also in palette/VRAM (+ its mirrored top)/OAM (planted: a VRAM mirror bug caught) → 2.99/2.69/4.65/2.58/5.12/16.50. (11) **NEON drawing** (`core/arch/arm64/ppu_neon.{h,c}`): the compositor (`ppu_compose_neon`, 16 pixels at a time without windows/effects, 8 with; `ppu_compose_generic` the twin) and whole 4bpp tile rows (`ppu_tile_row4_neon`, palette by table lookup); 7a's `test_speedups.c` compares them with the pre-7a code on the phone (planted: darken→brighten and a dropped flip, both caught) → **game A 4.01, game B 2.88, game C 5.99, game D 2.87, m7 5.60, snd1 23.35** (all hashes ok).
  (12) A probe (not kept) counted the instructions a64_slow runs: MUL (game A 41 % of them), ARM halfword/signed transfers (game B ~70 %), Thumb MUL and BX (game D 37 %), I/O loads/stores. Added to the asm: ARM MUL/MLA (+ `mul_carry` called for the S forms), Thumb MUL, LDRH/STRH/LDRSB/LDRSH, ARM BX and Thumb BX (a BX into the other state returns 2 to `a64_cpu_run`: on in the other loop without a cpu_step); planted bugs caught: MLA's extra cycle, the misaligned LDRSH, Thumb BX's refill → **game A 4.52, game B 3.33, game C 6.25, game D 3.20, m7 5.76, snd1 24.35**. Found: `dev.sh phone_bench_push` ignored a failed build and ran the old program (from 7a: `phone_bench_build` inside an `&&` chain, where `set -e` does not apply) — now `phone_bench_build || return 1` (goes into the plan).
  (13) The prefetch stall in asm (bus_prefetch_stall's loop; `stall_s` cached with the window; planted: a 7-halfword buffer, caught) and an AL shortcut in the ARM dispatch → 4.64/3.45/6.40/3.36/5.80/24.36. (14) ARM LDR/STR(B) specialised by bits 25-20 (64 copies; planted: up/down swapped, caught) → **game A 4.65, game B 3.52, game C 6.40, game D 3.36, m7 5.89, snd1 24.33**.
  **Tried and left out:** (a) *remembered instructions* — a cache in the ARM dispatch (opcode + handler per address, checked against the opcode, so never stale) made it slower (game B 3.52 → 3.40, all others down 0.5-2 %): in the threaded loop the decode is already one table lookup, so the cache's own loads cost more than they save; reverted. (b) *NEON sound* — the core's sound is 1-4 % of the time (`apu_on_sample`, `psg_advance`), the app's resampler 0.30 % of a small core (timed: 60 s of 32768 → 48000 Hz stereo in 0.183 s): both under the 5 % line.
  (15) **Found on the real phone:** `MotionTest.sensorValuesReachTheCoreWithinAFrame` gave the game only 100 ms after `start()`; run alone it failed 8-9 of 10 times on phone B — **with `main`'s (7a) code too** (in the full suite an earlier test has the sound running already, so it passed there). It now waits up to 2 s for the first frame (`seenSoon`), then 100 ms for the second values; 10/10 on both builds.

- **M10c brainstorm (2026-10-04)** — on new branch `milestone-10c`. The user's answers: phones only; saves + states; Dropbox first proposed, then **the user asked to also offer Google Drive** (more users, comes with every Google account) → both in 10c, Drive built first; automatic; ask on a clash; approach 1 (per game, file by file, receipt last). Checked online before the design: Drive's `drive.appdata` scope is **non-sensitive** (so the consent screen can go to In production without review — the 7-day refresh-token limit is only for Testing apps); Android sign-in is Play services' `AuthorizationClient` (`play-services-auth`, not yet in the Gradle cache → download needs the user's OK); Dropbox supports PKCE with refresh tokens and app links. The emulator image is Google APIs (Play services, no Play Store). Spec written and committed. Plan-time decisions: Dropbox's tokens and the waiting PKCE verifier in `files/sync/dropbox.json`, the phone id / last sync / stopped in `files/sync/cloud.json` (the spec §4 updated); Google Drive uploads create a new file then delete older same-name ones (no PATCH, which `HttpURLConnection` can't send); a paused game (Home) only uploads a snapshot; the game being played is locked (`GameLock`); `&` in URLs only through `Net.form` (UiTextTest).

- **M13 Task 9 (2026-10-05, `623d0bc`) — fix: the BIOS twin tests; BitUnPack and the affine sets exact.** `tests/unit/test_hle_twins.c`: every built-in BIOS routine against the user's real BIOS on random inputs (skipped without `tests/bios/gba_bios.bin`). Identical already: CpuSet, CpuFastSet, LZ77 W/V, RL W/V, Diff8 W/V, Diff16, Div, DivArm, Sqrt, ArcTan, ArcTan2, RegisterRamReset. Fixed: **BitUnPack** (a unit the offset overflows spills into the next — no masking; was 36/80 wrong), **ObjAffineSet / BgAffineSet** (the BIOS's sine table is cut toward zero, `trunc(16384·sin(2πi/256))`, ours was rounded; was 105/120 and 52/60 wrong, ±1–2 in the matrix) + BgAffineSet's arithmetic in 32-bit wrap-around (a UBSan overflow). No fingerprint changed. The twins also measured the routines' **speed** (built-in mostly much faster: LZ77 to VRAM 1,146 vs 19,888 cycles, RegisterRamReset 97 vs 257,192, CpuSet 2,590 vs 4,086 — table in plan Task 9) → next task. Unit 433/433, CTest 121/121, bench ok (snd1_demo ±6 % noise), on-device 78/78.

Fail-first test: a `str` loop past the cartridge header (the fast loop skips the first 0x100 bytes) — no note before, 5,373 after. Unit 434/434, CTest 121/121, bench ok, on-device 78/78.

- **M14 Task 5 — the speed check `gbabench --ra <n>` (2026-10-06, commit `39a81aa`)** — gbabench builds the glue + rcheevos; `--ra n` signs in to the pretend server, loads n made-up achievements (two 32-bit compares each, practically never true) and calls `ra_do_frame` after every frame. The phone emulator was running, so measured A/B alternately (2 rounds × 3 runs, plain then `--ra 300`); the PC was faster than at the M13 baseline anyway. Best of the two rounds, plain → `--ra 300`: game_a 1434.7 → 1398.7 fps, game_b 1144.8 → 1130.0, game_c 2152.5 → 2092.2, game_d 1186.5 → 1165.7, m7_demo 2672.0 → 2616.5, snd1_demo 7086.0 → 6657.5 — **every scene above its 97 % floor** (1161 / 921 / 1733 / 935 / 2202 / 5836); all picture and sound hashes `ok` in every run. The 300-achievement cost is ≈ 10 µs a frame (snd1 shows it most, ~6 %, because its frames are so cheap; at 60 fps it is < 0.1 % of a frame).

- **M14 Task 6 — the game loop and the JNI bridge (2026-10-06, commit `6707c25`)** — `emu_thread`: `emu_options_t.ra`; `ra_do_frame` after every `gba_run_frame` (fast-forward frames too), with a pending progress reset after a state load or the end of a rewind; while paused the thread wakes once a second for `ra_idle` under a new **core lock** (lock order: core lock, then the small lock; the paused thread never holds both); `core_free` replaced by `core_take` / `core_give` for state save / load, cheats, the frame copy, peeks and the new `emu_ra_idle`; hardcore limits in C: state loads refused, rewinding ignored, cheats cleared (`GBA_ERR_CHEAT` if any were asked for). `jni_shared.h` + `jni_ra.c` (one `ra_t` for the app's life, `pthread_once`; text as UTF-8 bytes); `jni_bridge.c` gives the session the `ra_t` and unloads the RA game in `stop_session` — **so the app must call `raLoadGame` after `loadRom`** (Task 10's `open` does: checked). `NativeBridge.ra*` declared; new on-device test `retroAchievementsIsBuiltIn`. `dev.sh apk` no C warnings; on-device **79/79**; CTest **121/121**.

- **Whole-repo audit (2026-10-23, no code changed)** — the user decided the app will be free and open source (docs commit `3cd8aa9`) and asked for a review with the **ponytail** skill: installed from github.com/DietrichGebert/ponytail (commit `09c434a`, MIT) as the six skill files only, in `~/.claude/skills/ponytail*` — **not** the plugin's hooks, status band or usage log (the user can add those with `/plugin marketplace add DietrichGebert/ponytail` + `/plugin install ponytail@ponytail` in a terminal `claude`). Five read-only reviewers by area, every finding re-checked; the report `docs/reviews/2026-10-23-ponytail-audit.md` (20 findings + 11 smaller ones listed). Verdict: the emulator itself is solid (no memory errors in graphics, sound or the assembly); the risks are untrusted files (states, backups, skins, picked files), two ways to lose a save, and going-public hygiene (no LICENSE, personal details in docs and history).

- **Audit fix 1 — save states are checked inside (2026-10-24, branch `audit-fixes`)** — the user said "fix 1, 2, 3 and 5". `core/src/state.c`: `values_ok` refuses a state whose memory-picking values are out of range (save type, Flash bank ≤ 1, EEPROM reply bit ≤ 68, RTC bit ≤ 7, square-wave steps ≤ 7 and wave sample ≤ 63, FIFO read ≤ 31 / count ≤ 32, sound ring read < 2048 / count ≤ 2048), whose next line end isn't due within a frame (the frame would never end) or with any event more than a frame overdue (endless catching up), or a `now` past half the 64-bit range; the ROM's size and CRC stay the loaded ROM's; `sched_refresh` works out the cached next event again. Stop mode keeps its events (time just stands still), so real states always pass. Tests red first (refused 0 of 14 bad fields, ROM size taken from the file, stale `next`), then green: `state_with_values_out_of_range_is_refused`, `state_keeps_the_loaded_roms_size`, `state_rebuilds_the_schedulers_next_event`. Unit **477/477**, CTest **121/121** (old states and the 32 fingerprint replays still load). **Follow-up** (an automatic security review of the commit flagged two more): the memory timing table is rebuilt from WAITCNT after a load (`bus_rebuild_timing`; a state with all-zero cycles would make time stand still) and the ROM's title and code stay the loaded ROM's (a state could drop their NUL); tests `state_rebuilds_the_memory_timing_from_waitcnt`, `state_keeps_the_loaded_roms_title_and_code`, red first; unit **479/479**, CTest **121/121**. (Fix 1's commit: `8b48868`.)

- **Audit fix 2 — a save that doesn't fit is kept (2026-10-24)** — `emu_thread.c` `load_save`: a `.sav` that doesn't fit the chip is copied to `<id>.sav.misfit` (`save_file_replace`) before the game starts on a blank chip, so the game's own saves (which replace the file and then its `.bak`) can't destroy it; `Backup.kt` takes `saves/<id>.sav.misfit` into backups (the user can get it from the `.zip`); the toast now says "This game's save file doesn't fit it, so the game starts without it. The file is kept and goes into your backups." Tests red first: on-device `SaveTest.aMisfitSaveIsKeptAsideBeforeTheGameSavesOverIt` (a 64 KiB save for the 32 KiB SRAM game, two plays; failed with no `.misfit` file), JVM `BackupTest.aBackupHoldsExactlyTheAppsParts` (the `.misfit` file in the list). Kotlin **236/236**, on-device **89/89**. **Emulator note:** the first launch of the phone emulator this session died silently (no process, `dev.sh androidtest` waited forever); the user said to reboot it — cold-booted with `-no-snapshot-load`, up in 24 s.

- **Audit fix 3 — a big picked file no longer crashes the app (2026-10-24)** — `MainActivity.readBytes` (read the whole picked file, then checked its size) is gone: `readPicked(uri, read)` with `SaveFiles.read` (stops past 128 KiB, the biggest save) for Import save and the in-game import, `Bios.read` (stops past 16 KiB) for the BIOS; too big → null → "Could not read that file." Tests red first (unresolved `read`), then green: `SaveFilesTest.aPickedFileIsReadOnlyUpToTheBiggestSave`, `BiosTest.aPickedFileIsReadOnlyUpToABiosSize` (an endless stream: null, read ≤ cap + 8 KiB). Kotlin **238/238**. (Fix 2's commit: `b98c9f0`.)

- **Audit fix 5 — a failed save write is tried again (2026-10-24)** — `emu_thread.c` `write_save`: on a failed write the save timer is set again (`save_timer_frame(…, true)`), so the write is retried about every second and at pause / stop until it works (the toast still shows once per game). Test red first: on-device `SaveTest.aFailedWriteIsTriedAgain` (the save's folder missing while the game saves, made afterwards; pause wrote nothing before the fix). On-device **90/90**, no C warnings. (Fix 3's commit: `40b9b16`.)

- **Audit fixes merged (2026-10-24)** — the user said "merge it into main": `git merge --no-ff refs/heads/audit-fixes` (`a03c02f`); on `main`: unit 479/479, CTest 121/121, Kotlin 238/238 (on-device 90/90 and `games --check` 31/31 ran on the branch just before). Branch `audit-fixes` deleted.

- **Audit fixes, round 2 (2026-10-24, branch `audit-fixes-2`)** — the user: "use the ponytail skill for all coding and code review" (CLAUDE.md rule) and "start working on fixes 5-20" (5 was already done). One commit per finding, test-first where there is logic:
  - **7** a bad backup can't crash a restore: `Backup.unpack` refuses any `backup.json`, `prefs/` or `boxart/` entry over 1 MiB (read into memory) and turns any Throwable (a StackOverflowError from a deeply nested file) into NotABackup; `Restore.swapOrRollBack` rolls back on any Throwable. Tests red first: `BackupTest.aFileTheAppReadsWholeIsRefusedOverOneMegabyte`, `aDeeplyNestedFileIsRefusedNotACrash`. Kotlin 240/240.
  - **8** a broken Delta skin can't crash the app: `DeltaSkin.face` refuses a layout size that isn't finite and above 0, or thinner than 1:20 (a 0 × 0 skin imported fine, then made the game screen crash on every open); `parse` turns a StackOverflowError (nested too deep) into "not a skin"; the import thread catches any Throwable (a picture too big to decode). Tests red first in `DeltaSkinTest.problems`. Kotlin 240/240.
  - **9** a release build: `bash tools/dev.sh apk-release` (`:app:assembleRelease`) → `app/build/outputs/apk/release/`; signed with the key named in the gitignored `keystore.properties` (storeFile, storePassword, keyAlias, keyPassword), else unsigned (`app-release-unsigned.apk`); release builds are not debuggable (checked with `aapt2 dump badging`: no `application-debuggable`; the debug APK has it), so `run-as` can't read `files/ra.json` and the pretend server can't be switched on. `.gitignore`: `keystore.properties`, `*.jks`, `*.keystore`. **The key itself is not made** (it needs passwords): the user makes it once with `keytool -genkeypair -v -keystore gbaemu-release.jks -keyalg RSA -keysize 4096 -validity 10000 -alias gbaemu` and keeps it + its passwords safe (losing it means no more updates under the same app id). No minify (not needed).
  - **10** cheats don't control each other: at the end of `cheat_decode` an Action Replay block left open (no "end if") is closed — it guards the rest of its own cheat — and every condition's then / else counts are cut to the cheat's own steps. Test red first: `cheat_conditions_stay_inside_their_own_cheat` (a lone CodeBreaker condition, an open AR block, a plain write; the block's code ran with its condition false). Unit 480/480.
  - **11** one folder for games and automatic backups: `useFolder` no longer gives back the old games folder's read permission when it is also the backup folder; `autoBackup` counts the backup as made once it is written, and the clean-up of old ones has its own `try` (a failed clean-up used to report "failed", skip `lastAuto`, and so make a new full backup on every quit with none deleted). **No test:** both are inside MainActivity, which has no test harness (to check by hand: the same folder for both, change the games folder, quit a game → a backup and no "failed" note). Kotlin builds.
  - **12** saves from mGBA for games with a clock load: mGBA adds a 16-byte clock footer (game A: 131,088 bytes; confirmed on mGBA's forums); `gba_load_saveram` leaves it out (our clock follows the phone's), `SaveFiles.validSize` / `read` accept chip size + 16. Tests red first: `save_load_saveram_takes_mgbas_saves_with_a_clock_footer`, `SaveFilesTest` sizes + an game A-size read. Unit 481/481, Kotlin 240/240.
  - **13** a byte write to a timer's reload value keeps the reload's other byte (`io.c` `write8` merged with `read16`, which gives the live counter). Test red first: `timer_reload_byte_writes_keep_the_other_byte` (0x129B instead of 0x1234). Unit 482/482, CTest 121/121 (the mGBA suite's timer scores are in CTest).
  - **14** achievements that watch save memory work before the first save: `emu_thread.c` `memory_of` gives rcheevos the whole 64 KiB save window when the chip's size isn't known yet (an EEPROM game with no save, an SRAM game still "unknown") — rcheevos checks the addresses once at game start and switched those achievements off for the session; the core's save buffer is always 128 KiB, so the bytes exist (blank 0xFF). No chip (`GBA_SAVE_NONE`) still gives none. **No test** (emu_thread isn't in the PC build; an on-device test would need a save-memory achievement on the pretend server). `dev.sh apk` no warnings.
  - **15** `.gitignore` now keeps out `tests/bios/`, `*.zip` (the user's games come zipped), `*.wav` (the test runner's `--wav`) and `gbaview-*.png` (the PC viewer's F12 screenshots land in the repo root); checked: no tracked file matches, and test files of each kind are ignored.
  - **16** `core/CMakeLists.txt`: the ARM64 assembly is built only on Android or Linux (it is ELF-only: a Mac with Apple Silicon or Windows on ARM now builds the plain-C core) and not with `GBA_NOTES` (it skips the warning log's notes, so `notes_see_palette_stores_from_the_fast_cpu_loop` and the game check would differ on Linux ARM64); the `GBA_NOTES` option now comes first. Checked: the app's arm64 build still has the assembly (`cpu_a64` in its `build.ninja`); the phone's unit-test build has no `GBA_NOTES`. Unit 482/482.
  - **17** `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties` pins `distributionSha256Sum=ed1a8d68…911d7c` for `gradle-8.14.3-all.zip` (the same value on `services.gradle.org/…/gradle-8.14.3-all.zip.sha256` and gradle.org's release-checksums page); a fresh clone's wrapper now refuses a changed download. The build still runs.
  - **18** `FolderScan` remembers, while the app runs, whether each zip (by key + size) holds a game, so a big zip of other consoles' games is read once per app start instead of on every return to the app (`shortcut:` comment: not kept across restarts); a zip that can't be opened is tried again next time. Not done (named): skipping a scan while one runs — `scanRun` already drops a stale scan's answer, and with zips remembered an overlapping scan is cheap. **No JVM test** (needs a ContentResolver); the on-device `LibraryDeviceTest` covers the scan.
  - **19 (half)** choosing a box picture works on a background thread (`boxChosen`: the game's id, the up-to-50 MiB read and the shrink off the UI thread; the library and the toast back on it). **Not done:** `open()` still reads and unzips the ROM on the UI thread — moving it changes the game-start flow (Continue's insert animation, `waitForAchievements`), so it waits for a phone-emulator check. **No test** (MainActivity); Kotlin builds.
  - **20 (part 1)** one `File.writeSafely` (new `SafeWrite.kt`: `.tmp` next to the file, folders made, swapped in; test `SafeWriteTest`, red first) replaces the copied temp-and-rename code in `Library`, `LayoutStore`, `Cheats`, `BoxArt` (×2), `BoxArtHelper`, `RaAccount`, `Bios`, `SaveFiles.import` (the `.bak` copy now made just before) — and `BackupSchedule.save` now writes safely too (it wrote in place: a damaged file switched automatic backups off silently); `Net.get` is built on `Net.send`; the unused `NativeBridge.romTitle` / `emu_rom_title` deleted. Kotlin 242/242, `dev.sh apk` no warnings.
  - **20 (part 2)** `core/src/mem/bus.c` uses `bus_inline.h`'s `bus_load_n` / `bus_access_cycles` / `bus_code_ptr` instead of its own copies (where code may be read straight from memory is now written once); the button-script reader moved to `tools/common/key_script.{c,h}` (testrunner's growing version — the bench copy stopped at 512 events, `game_d` has 504) and both tools use it; `tools/build_test_roms.py` imports `find_bin_dir` / `tool` from `gen_bios_stub.py`. One commit each, test-first where there is logic:
  - **1** Export save after the app was restarted while the picker was open: `writeExport` deletes the empty file the picker made (`DocumentsContract.deleteDocument`) and says "The export didn't finish. Export the save again." (it used to leave a 0-byte `.sav` without a word). **No test** (inside MainActivity). Kotlin builds.
  - **2** an automatic backup and a restore / undo take turns: `Restore.backUp`, `apply` and `undo` are `@Synchronized` (a restore confirmed while the backup after a quit was still writing could mix the two phones' data in that backup; the "Restoring" spinner now simply waits). Test red first: `RestoreTest.aRestoreWaitsForABackupThatIsStillBeingWritten` (a backup stream held mid-write; the restore finished meanwhile). Kotlin 243/243.
  - **3** an Action Replay "else" right after its block "if" no longer flips the block: `decode.c` remembers the else in `block_else` (it used to read `then_n == 0` as "no else"). Test red first: `cheat_ar_else_right_after_its_if`. Unit 483/483.
  - **4** `render_create` checks `pthread_create`: if the drawing thread can't start (the phone very low on memory) it cleans up and returns NULL — every caller already copes with no renderer — instead of the UI waiting forever in `render_set_window` and `render_destroy` joining a thread that never existed. **No test** (an Android thread failure can't be forced in the tests). `dev.sh apk` no warnings.
  - **5** a PDF skin keeps one drawn copy per way round: `SkinPicture.kept` deletes the older `cache-<p|l>-*.png` once a new size is drawn (every new view size — split screen, display size — used to add a full-screen PNG for good), and backups leave `cache-` files out (`Backup.files`; they are drawn again; restoring an old backup that has them still works). Tests red first: on-device `SkinPictureTest.aPdfIsDrawnAtTheAskedSizeSeeThroughWhereEmptyAndKept` (a second size), JVM `BackupTest.aBackupHoldsExactlyTheAppsParts` (a cache file on the phone). Kotlin 243/243.

Checked: the default still builds; `JBR=/nowhere` fails with Gradle's "JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory".

## Notes / context
- **The Game Boy core (17a, 2026-11-12) — what a new session needs:** `core_gb/` (API `include/gb.h`, tests' API `gb_debug.h`; `src/`: `cpu.c` SM83, `bus.c` memory map + `gb_tick` (one machine cycle: 4 clock ticks, 2 at double speed), `timer.c`, `serial.c` (+ joypad), `cart.c` (MBC1/2/3/5), `rtc.c` (clock + the save file), `ppu.c` (timing) + `ppu_draw.c` (line renderer, both consoles), `apu.c`, `cgb.c`, `state.c`); `machine/` (one handle over both cores; `machine_gba()` / `machine_gb()` give the core for one-console features); `core_common/` (rewind ring + packer, both cores). Timing model: every bus access first ticks the hardware (`gb_tick`), then reads / writes. Test ROMs: `python tools/fetch_test_roms.py` → `tests/roms/gb/{blargg,mooneye,acid}`; the list that must pass is `tests/gb_tests.json` (CTest `gb_*`, 135 ROMs); picture fingerprints `gb_*` in `tests/expected_frames.json` (run plain, through a state and through a rewind). A dev aid kept only in the 17a session's scratchpad re-surveyed every ROM and rewrote `gb_tests.json`; to redo it, loop `testrunner <rom> --gb-serial Passed | --gb-memtext Passed | --gb-mooneye` over the folders. **Known not passing (by design or time-boxed):** blargg dmg_sound 09 / 10 / 12 and cgb_sound 10 (wave channel timed inside a cycle), oam_bug (all), mooneye ppu `intr_2_mode0_timing_sprites` and `lcdon_write_timing-GS`, `multicart_rom_8Mb`, every test for another model (dmg0, mgb, sgb, sgb2, S, A, cgb0) and the mooneye `misc/` tests (a Game Boy Color running a plain game in compatibility mode — this core runs plain games as an original Game Boy). Screen timing numbers and their source are in the comment at the top of `ppu.c`. Speed numbers: the 17a Task 11 entry under Completed.
- **Bash tool trap (found 2026-11-12):** a heredoc that contains an odd number of apostrophes (C character literals, "blargg's") makes the whole command fail to parse ("unexpected EOF while looking for matching `'`") and nothing runs — write such scripts with the Write tool and run the file.
- **Release prep (researched 2026-10-23; decided since in milestone 16's spec — GitHub only, no Play Store, so (2) no longer applies; (3) and RetroAchievements re-checked 2026-11-12 in the spec's §2):** (1) **RetroAchievements approval** ([hardcore compliance requirements](https://docs.retroachievements.org/general/hardcore-compliance-requirements.html)): the emulator must have been **public for at least 6 months** first; a privacy policy (data kept how long, server countries, GDPR) — placeholders or contradictions are an auto-fail; a page listing every shipped open-source part and its licence (rcheevos, MIT); a unique User-Agent with a numeric, rising version (today `Net.kt` says `GBAEmulator/1.0` while `versionName` is `0.1` — line them up); **offline unlocks must be securely cached and sent when the connection returns** — rcheevos retries only while the app runs, so unlocks made offline are probably lost when the app closes: likely work; save files in standard formats; Rich Presence and leaderboards on; hardcore visibly shown while playing; the hardcore limits (no state loads, rewind, slow-down, cheats; switching to hardcore resets the game) — ours meet these. (2) **Google Play** (if chosen): $25 once + ID check; a personal account made after 2023-11-13 needs a **closed test with ≥ 12 testers opted in for 14 days in a row** before production; a privacy policy URL and the Data safety form (internet: RetroAchievements, box art); an AAB signed with a key that must be kept safe; screenshots must not show copyrighted games (use the M13 homebrew games, if their licences allow); no Nintendo / Game Boy names in the title or icon. (3) **Android developer verification:** since 2026-09-30 in Brazil, Indonesia, Singapore and Thailand (worldwide in 2027) certified phones only install apps from verified developers, even outside the Play Store — so a GitHub / F-Droid release needs the developer verified too (else users face a 24-hour "advanced flow"). (4) **Open source:** licence chosen: GPL-3.0-or-later (2026-10-24, `LICENSE`); the git history holds only the project's own test ROMs (`tests/own_roms/*.gba`), no games, BIOS or user screenshots (checked 2026-10-23). Box art downloaded from libretro's thumbnails is a legal grey area, common among emulators.
- **RetroAchievements approval (the user, from 2027-05-12 — six months after DreamBoy went public on 2026-11-12):** ask RetroAchievements to approve the client (User-Agent `DreamBoy/<version> (Android …) rcheevos/12.5`) so hardcore unlocks count as hardcore; until then its server sends a "Warning: Unknown Emulator" (shown as a notice) and refuses hardcore unlocks. After approval, turn the Hardcore default back on (`Settings.hardcore = true`, and `SettingsTest`). **Leaderboards (M15)** run only in hardcore (rcheevos), so real score submissions also start with that approval — nothing in the app needs changing; check a real submission then (the rank card, and a refused score's "Couldn't send a score" card).
- **RetroAchievements on the emulator (M14):** the debug app (`com.tengen.dreamboy.debug` since milestone 16; the old `com.tengen.gbaemu` debug app with the user's real sign-in is still installed on the emulator) uses the pretend server only while `files/ra-fake` exists (`MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 adb shell run-as com.tengen.dreamboy.debug touch files/ra-fake`, then force-stop and launch); its account is `Tester` / `secret` (a value of this project's pretend server; nothing leaves the phone); it gives every game a 5-achievement demo set that unlocks by itself over 40 s. Remove `files/ra-fake`, `files/ra.json` and `files/ra/` (its placeholder badges share names with real ones) before using the real server. Drive the emulator by element text (a uiautomator dump: tap the element whose text matches), never blind coordinates — a blind tap once landed in a running game. Known small gap: an activity restart (e.g. a font-size change) turns "Sign in again" into "Not signed in" (the expired token was already forgotten).
- **rcheevos facts (read in its 12.5.0 code while planning M14, 2026-10-06):** GBA hash = MD5 of the whole ROM file (`rc_hash_buffer`, ≤ 64 MiB). GBA memory map: 0x00000–0x07FFF IWRAM, 0x08000–0x47FFF EWRAM, 0x48000–0x57FFF save RAM. With `rc_client_set_allow_background_memory_reads(0)` a game load finishes (activates; its callback fires) only inside `rc_client_idle` / `rc_client_do_frame`. Only unlocks (and leaderboard entries) are retried: first at once, then 1 s, 2 s, … 120 s; a sign-in or game load fails at once without internet. DISCONNECTED / RECONNECTED events are raised in `rc_client_idle`. "Mastered" (GAME_COMPLETED) is raised on the frame after an award answer says `AchievementsRemaining: 0` — the pretend servers must count. Achievements start WAITING and must be false once before they can fire, but one with a hit target arms itself (the hit count isn't reached on the first frame). `rc_client_can_pause`: allowed, then not again until ~20 unpaused frames (more after repeated pauses, at most 5 s). rcheevos' own test answers (`test/test_rc_client.c` in the release) are the model for the pretend servers. It compiles warning-free with the project's clang (`-std=c11`, needs `-pthread` on mingw).
- **The BIOS twin tests (M13):** `tests/unit/test_hle_twins.c` and `test_hle_sound.c` run each built-in BIOS routine against the user's real BIOS (`tests/bios/gba_bios.bin`, path from the `GBA_TEST_BIOS` define) and skip without it. Trick: a machine boots with the built-in BIOS (no boot animation), then the real BIOS is copied into `bus.bios` and `hle_bios` set false, so `swi` runs the real code. They print both machines' cycles per call too.
- **Comparing with the real BIOS in whole games:** `--bios tests/bios/gba_bios.bin` makes the real BIOS play its boot animation first, so the game starts **272 frames later** than with the built-in BIOS (measured with hack 2) — shift a button script by 272 frames before comparing runs.
- **Speed A/B when the PC is noisy (M13):** `git worktree add build/ab_old_src <old commit>`, configure it as `build/release` is (`-DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DGBA_SANITIZE=OFF`), build target `gbabench`, run both gbabench alternately with their own `tests/bench/scenes.txt`; remove the worktree after. The phone emulator running in the background slows every PC scene ~40 %.
- **The game check (M13):** the public list `tests/games/games.txt` plus the user's own games in the gitignored `tests/games/games.local.txt` (same format; the speed scenes likewise in `tests/bench/scenes.local.txt`); `bash tools/dev.sh games` (all), `games --only <name>`, `games --check`, `games --lock <name|all>`; the list `tests/games/games.txt` (format in its header); per-run files in `build/check/work/<name>/` (`shots/`, `sound.wav`, `keys.txt`, `output.txt`), the report `build/check/report/index.html` + `<name>.png` contact sheets (gitignored: they show the user's games). Tool tests: `python tools/test_game_check.py`.
- **M15 speed with RetroAchievements + leaderboards (2026-10-06, `bash tools/dev.sh bench --runs 3 --ra 300` = 300 achievements + 30 boards, phone emulator running, best of 2 rounds):** game_a 1419.2 fps, game_b 1174.6, game_c 2203.5, game_d 1183.8, m7_demo 2704.0, snd1_demo 6916.6 — all above the M13 floors; plain in the same session: 1408.2 / 1160.4 / 2206.8 / 1230.1 / 2925.5 / 7265.6.
- **M14 speed with RetroAchievements (2026-10-06, `bash tools/dev.sh bench --runs 3 --ra 300`, phone emulator running):** game_a 1398.7 fps, game_b 1130.0, game_c 2092.2, game_d 1165.7, m7_demo 2616.5, snd1_demo 6657.5 — all above the M13 floors below; plain in the same session: 1434.7 / 1144.8 / 2152.5 / 1186.5 / 2672.0 / 7086.0.
- **M13 PC speed baseline (2026-10-05, `bash tools/dev.sh bench --runs 3`, before any milestone 13 core change):** game_a 1197.1 fps, game_b 949.6, game_c 1787.0, game_d 964.1, m7_demo 2270.4, snd1_demo 6016.8. **97 % floors** (spec §6): game_a 1161, game_b 921, game_c 1733, game_d 935, m7_demo 2202, snd1_demo 5836. PC numbers vary a few % between runs: a scene below its floor is measured again before it counts.
- **impeccable (design skill, from M12):** `PRODUCT.md` (product truth) and `DESIGN.md` (the design system, + `.impeccable/design.json`) at the repo root; review screenshots go in `.impeccable/review/` (gitignored: they show the user's games); the direction contract in `.impeccable/surfaces/…screens-kt.md` (read it with `impeccable surface-brief read app/src/main/java/com/tengen/gbaemu/Screens.kt`); `DESIGN.md` + `.impeccable/design.json` get written at the end by the documenter agent from the finished build. No image generation on this machine, so builds are code-led (no comps). It is a native app: the web detector doesn't apply; the finish review uses emulator screenshots in `.impeccable/review/` (`phone.png`, …). The decision page runs on `127.0.0.1` and is opened in the browser pane; `serve-question --wait` returns exit 3 every 60 s until the user answers (loop it in the background).
- **Backups (11):** `files/backup-state.json` (automatic folder URI, `lastAuto`, `lastBackup`; not in backups), `files/restore/` (`incoming/` while a restore waits, `safety.zip` = the Undo copy). To move automatic-backup time on the emulator: force-stop, push a `backup-state.json` to `/data/local/tmp`, `chmod 644`, `run-as com.tengen.dreamboy.debug cp … files/`. In Git Bash set `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` for `adb shell` paths (else `/sdcard` becomes `C:/Program Files/Git/sdcard`); outside `dev.sh`, `adb` is `$LOCALAPPDATA/Android/Sdk/platform-tools/adb.exe`.
- **No integrations, no app keys (user decision 2026-10-04, cancelling 10c):** saves and states stay on the phone; users back them up themselves. Don't propose cloud sync, sign-ins with third-party services, or anything needing an app key or client id. (10b's box art downloads from libretro need no key or account, so they stay.) **One exception (user decision 2026-10-05): RetroAchievements** (milestone 14) — the user's own RA sign-in, token kept, never the password; rcheevos needs no app key.
- **The user's own games** are listed only in the gitignored `tests/games/games.local.txt` and `tests/bench/scenes.local.txt` (never in the repository).
- Decisions made in brainstorming (M2): PNG screenshots + no-install Win32 viewer `gbaview.exe`; DMA + timers pulled from M3 into M2; correctness = pixel-exact scene unit tests + inspected screenshots locked as fingerprints in `tests/expected_frames.json`; line-by-line layered renderer.
- **Tonc download (2026-09-25):** coranac.com's HTTPS resets connections, and it refused Python's default User-Agent, so `tools/fetch_test_roms.py` uses `http://` + a User-Agent + a pinned SHA-256 (`f172fc93…50ba`, set from two independent downloads that matched). The server was down for a while (7+ resets) and then worked; if it fails, just rerun later. All 19 demos are in `tests/roms/tonc/` (gitignored).
- **Box art (10b):** the app keeps `files/boxart/` (`names.tsv` with a `#time` first line, `cheat-files.txt`, `boxart.json`, `<id>.jpg`); deleting the folder makes it download again. Airplane mode on the emulator: `adb shell cmd connectivity airplane-mode enable|disable`.
- **Test cheat files:** `tests/cht/` (gitignored, never committed) holds libretro's game A `.cht` (downloaded with the user's OK 2026-10-04). The PC test runner checks a cheat with `bash tools/dev.sh run <rom> --frames 1 --cheat <file>` ("line N is not a code" when unreadable).
- **History rewrite (2026-11-12):** before going public, the triple check found the user's two phone models and their model codes across the docs, `PRODUCT.md` and `BackupTextTest.kt` since the first commit, and the package name of another app on the shared emulator in `docs/PROGRESS.md`. With the user's OK the whole history (36 commits and the `v1.0.0` tag) was rewritten with `git filter-branch` (a scrub script: the phones became **"phone A"** (the newer one, Android 16) and **"phone B"** (the older 2018 one, Android 11), the app name became "another app"), commit messages too; every commit ID changed and the IDs quoted in this file were swapped for the new ones. Never write the phone models or other apps' names into the repo again. The words the scanner looks for (the user's email, the phone's serial, the RetroAchievements user name) are in the gitignored `tests/public_scan.local.txt`. A bundle of the pre-scrub history was kept only in this session's scratchpad.
- **Branches and tags:** this repo started 2026-10-24 with one commit on `main`; since 2026-11-12 its remote `origin` is `https://github.com/tengencode/DreamBoy` (public since 2026-11-12; recreated that day after the history rewrite) and its first tag is `v1.0.0` (DreamBoy's first release). Push with `git push origin main` (and tags with `git push origin <tag>`) only on the user's OK. The milestone branches and the tags `milestone-1` … `milestone-15` (each milestone merged into `main` with `git merge --no-ff`) are in the private archive `~/Documents/gba_emulator`, as are every commit hash quoted in this file from before 2026-10-24. Delete a merged branch with `git branch -d <name>`; use `refs/heads/<name>` in merges when a tag shares the name.
- **Deferred from M2 (by spec):** mid-scanline register changes, green swap, DMA3 video capture, sound-FIFO DMA timing, timer→FIFO link, ~~2-cycle timer start delay~~ (done in 3c-2), write-only registers read back their stored value (M3), a byte write to TMxCNT_H restarts timing (small gap; keeping the tick phase was tried in 3c-2 Task 8 — no suite effect, left out), one shared `EV_DMA` event.
- **Deferred from M1 (by plan):** decoded-block cache (M7), ~~ROM prefetch buffer~~ (done in 3c-2, mGBA's estimate), Stop treated as Halt, unmapped I/O reads return 0 (M3), no save chips (M3).
- **Execution mode:** always inline in this session (superpowers:executing-plans), never subagents — permanent user decision, in `CLAUDE.md`; don't ask. Plan checkboxes are ticked as tasks finish.
- **User preferences:** always layman mode; update all docs after every task — especially PROGRESS.md's status and Next — and confirm no document is stale; go through all of a plan's tasks automatically, stopping only when the user must check something on their end (all in `CLAUDE.md`, rules added by the user 2026-09-28).
- **Speed / build types (found 2026-09-25 with the user's game A hack):** the Debug + ASan/UBSan build in `build/host` runs a real game at only ~30-45 fps (too slow: choppy sound). The optimised build runs the same game at **558 fps headless** with an identical frame hash, and `gbaview` at a steady 60 fps with sound. So `bash tools/dev.sh view` now builds and uses `build/release` (Release, no sanitizers; configured automatically); `bash tools/dev.sh release` builds it alone. Tests (`unit`, `test`, `run`) still use the checked debug build.
- **The release viewer is `build/release/tools/viewer/gbaview.exe`** — close it before rebuilding (it locks the exe like the debug one).
- **A running `gbaview` locks `gbaview.exe`**: a build/test then fails to link it ("Permission denied"). Close viewer windows first (Claude closed one with PowerShell `CloseMainWindow()` after the user's check was done).
- **User runs commands in PowerShell**, where plain `bash` starts WSL (not installed) → "execvpe(/bin/bash) failed". Give the user commands as `& "C:\Program Files\Git\bin\bash.exe" tools/dev.sh <cmd>`.
- **`adb shell run-as <pkg> sh -c "…"` does not work** (found 2026-09-28): adb joins the words and the device shell splits them again ('syntax error: unexpected then'). Use plain `run-as <pkg> <command>` calls; `adb shell` passes the command's exit status back (e.g. `ls` of a missing file → 1).
- **Run tests only through `tools/dev.sh`** — running `build/host/.../unit_tests.exe` directly fails (it needs llvm-mingw's DLLs on PATH, which dev.sh adds).
- **Build:** `bash tools/dev.sh configure` once (already done; `build/host`, gitignored), then `bash tools/dev.sh unit [filter]` / `test` / `run`. `dev.sh` finds CMake/Ninja in the Android SDK and clang in the winget llvm-mingw folder.
- **Android build (from 4a):** `JBR` (Android Studio's Java) and `GBA_AVD` (the emulator) can be set from outside `dev.sh` (2026-10-24). Gradle 8.14.3 wrapper in the repo, run by `dev.sh` with Android Studio's JBR 21 and `--offline`; Android Gradle Plugin 8.13.0, Kotlin 2.2.21, Compose 1.11.4 / Material3 1.4.0. A Gradle background process may keep files locked after a build: `"$(ls -d ~/.gradle/wrapper/dists/gradle-8.14.3-all/*/gradle-8.14.3)/bin/gradle" --stop` (with JAVA_HOME = the JBR) releases them. Deep Android build folders can exceed Windows' path limit for `git worktree remove` — delete with Git Bash `rm -rf`, then `git worktree prune`.
- **The user's real phones:** phone A (Android 16, serial not recorded) and phone B (Android 11, serial not recorded; 7a's measuring phone). For milestone 7 the user allowed running `gbabench`/games in `/data/local/tmp`, installing the app and its tests, through `dev.sh`'s `phone-*` commands; never anything else without asking. Android's profiler (simpleperf) is refused on both for adb-shell programs.
- **Emulator cold boot (found 2026-09-28):** after Android inside the emulator crashes, `adb emu kill` + `dev.sh emu` resumes the saved (broken) quick-boot snapshot. Cold boot: kill it, wait until `qemu-system` is gone, then `"$LOCALAPPDATA/Android/Sdk/emulator/emulator" -avd Pixel_9_API_36_1 -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot-load` and wait for `sys.boot_completed` before any `dev.sh` command. Ask the user first (memory rule).
- **The app has no home-screen icon on the emulator** (adb installs aren't pinned by the Pixel launcher): open it from the app list (swipe up) or with `bash tools/dev.sh launch`; the user can pin it (press and hold → drag).
- **Phone emulator keys (found 2026-09-26):** the emulator window sends **letter keys as 0.1 ms down+up taps, even while held**; arrows and Enter arrive as real presses. The key latch (4b Task 5) makes the taps reach the game. Key map: arrows = D-pad, X = A, Z = B, A = L, S = R, Enter = Start, Backspace = Select (the user did not know it — worth showing in the app, 4c). `adb shell getevent -lt` (emulator serial!) shows what arrives.
- **Phone emulator keyboard:** the AVD `Pixel_9_API_36_1` now has `hw.keyboard=yes` (changed with the user's OK, 2026-09-26; backup `config.ini.bak-before-keyboard`) — needed for PC-keyboard play; `adb shell input keyevent` works either way. Copies on the emulator's Download folder (not in git): `m7_demo.gba`, `obj_demo.gba` and the user's `hack-1.gba`.
- **Machine:** Windows 11, Git Bash + PowerShell. Android SDK at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Android\Sdk` with NDK 27.2.12479018, CMake 3.31.6 (+ Ninja), emulator image android-36.1 **x86_64** (ARM assembly can't run in the emulator — needs a real phone later). Python 3.14, JDK 24.
- **Decisions:** thin Kotlin UI; cached interpreter + ARM64 asm (asm in milestone 7); built-in HLE BIOS + optional real BIOS; high-compatibility accuracy; arm64 + x86_64, Android 8+; emulation thread synced to audio.
- **The user's GBA BIOS** (for testing only, never committed): `tests/bios/gba_bios.bin`, 16384 bytes, CRC-32 `81977335` (gitignored by `*.bin`; added by the user 2026-09-28).
- **Test ROMs:** jsmolka/gba-tests pinned to commit `a7113b67e63f83a9b321696ddd7042ccfad6c881`; CPU-test results sit in `r12` (`r7` for thumb). All 7 jsmolka ROMs + 19 Tonc demos are downloaded (`python tools/fetch_test_roms.py`, gitignored). Approved frame fingerprints live in `tests/expected_frames.json` (add with `tools/approve_frame.py` only after inspecting the PNG). Never commit ROMs/BIOS files.
